| **M31** | pm-sprint (infra/observability) | **Position telemetry — the missing state between entry and exit** (operator-directed 2026-08-16) — the system holds ENTRY facts (`order_packages`), EXIT facts (`trades`, written at close), and per-pass transient recomputation, with **nothing in between**. `src/runtime/trail_decay.py:88-99` computes `peak_r` (MFE in R), `bars_since_peak`, entry and sized risk on EVERY exit-loop pass for EVERY open position on the donchian/pullback family — then collapses it to one boolean and discards the rest (or, undeclared, writes one row to a soak whose own docstring says "Nothing reads it back"). So a live trade's trajectory is computed roughly every 30s and thrown away. **Motivating use case:** answering "should we hold this 18-day XRP short?" required four diag pulls and a page of hand arithmetic to reconstruct open_R 2.88, cap_R 3.92, and reward:risk-from-here 1.04:1.46 — and MFE was not reconstructible at all. **Design:** [`docs/design/position-telemetry-DESIGN.md`](docs/design/position-telemetry-DESIGN.md) — one telemetry record per open trade, refreshed each exit pass, DERIVED from the same functions the levers use (not a second definition), `offloop_hooks`-instrumented, states never collapsed, provenance stamped. Phases: **P1** declare-time lever-reachability guard (Tier-1) · **P2** the record, observe-only (Tier-2) · **P3** readers — `/positions` R-fields, diag surface, a position-lifecycle banner kind, the exit-ladder input (Tier-1) · **P4** **backtest↔live MFE parity** (Tier-1) — the largest item, because every bug in the tp-cap family is "the harness measured a book production does not run" and there has never been a live measurement to check it against · **P5** any lever that READS telemetry (Tier-3). **Infrastructure, not edge** — it makes the exit-lever program checkable, which is where M20 keeps losing weeks. | 🔄 **IN PROGRESS** 2026-08-16 — **P1 DONE** (declare-time lever-reachability guard, #9614) · **P2 DONE and LIVE-VERIFIED** (#9614 + the `account_id` fix #9660): the table is populating on the live trader, **12 of 12** rows carry `account_id` across five accounts (`order_state: applied`), and the writer costs **6.4 ms mean / 55.1 ms max at n=306** in `offloop_hooks` — negligible against a ~23.6 s exit pass. Verification used `bot_uptime_s`/`process_started_utc`, **never `git_sha`** (which reads the working tree, not the running process). The motivating XRP trade is now fully attributed: `xrp_pullback_2h` trade 4163 on **`bybit_2` (real money)**, `peak_r 3.4179` vs `cap_r 3.9233` vs declared `arm_r 4.49` — the arm-above-cap defect readable off one row, on a trade whose MFE was previously not reconstructible at all. **P2 also shipped a defect I caught on the FIRST post-deploy read:** `account_id` was structurally unpopulatable (`order_packages` has no such column; the monitor has no account in scope) and **the tests could not have caught it** — they asserted a round-trip that worked; only the live journal showed the column was never fed (#9660). **Adjacent, merged:** the exit-mechanism coverage probe (#9633 — **zero orphaned declares over 46 of 47 RESOLVED legs**; `ict_scalp_5m` does not resolve and is ungraded) and its CI guard (#9679), which closes the gap where a sweep can bless a lever the leg's own unit module never reads (`backtest_trend.py:555` applies `stale_exit_bars` in the engine, not via `monitor()`). **P3 readers are now UNBLOCKED** — the table is confirmed populating, which was the prerequisite. **P4 backtest↔live MFE parity now has a NAMED, MEASURED consumer and is the binding blocker for it** (2026-08-16, #9712/#9730/#9739): a declared exit arm is a **volatility threshold** — `cap_R = 0.099/(atr_stop_mult × ATR/close)` — and the six p80 arms were swept **before the harness could model the TP cap**, so `trend_donchian` and `trend_donchian_sol_4h` (same family, same stop-mult) got arms 1.16× apart against ceilings **7.3× apart**. The sweep now grades its own proposal (`verdicts.json::p80_arm_reach`), but **only against the BACKTEST ceiling** — verified on a real artefact, `gld_pullback_1d` passes that check at 3.86R while being unreachable on the live book (0 of 8 entries). **Closing the remaining half requires exactly the live-population input P4 would supply**, so P4 is no longer just "the largest item", it is what the arm-reachability programme is waiting on. Memo: [`m20-arm-reachability-is-a-vol-threshold-2026-08-16.md`](docs/research/m20-arm-reachability-is-a-vol-threshold-2026-08-16.md). P5 remains Tier-3. Sprint logs: [`S-M31-POSITION-TELEMETRY-2026-08-16.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-M31-POSITION-TELEMETRY-2026-08-16.md) + [`S-M31-VERIFY-AND-COVERAGE-2026-08-16.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-M31-VERIFY-AND-COVERAGE-2026-08-16.md) + [`S-M20-LIVE-REACHABILITY-BASIS-2026-08-16.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-M20-LIVE-REACHABILITY-BASIS-2026-08-16.md) (the **live** `order_packages` reachability basis for the queued levers — `xrp_pullback_2h` **2/37 = 5.4% over a COMPLETE population** (the first read was 0/27 over 27 of 37 rows and inferred [0.0%, 27.0%] — the bound held but both reaching rows sat in the truncated tail), `trend_donchian_sol_4h` **0/16 over a COMPLETE population**, `scha_trend_long_1d` `total: 0` because the leg is 5 US trading sessions old; **nothing decided — all three stay `queued_tier3`**). Continued in [`S-M20-USO-LEVER-REACHABILITY-2026-08-16.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-M20-USO-LEVER-REACHABILITY-2026-08-16.md): **`uso_trend_1h` moved `unmeasured` → `reachable`/`ok`** on a COMPLETE live population (30/30 order packages, `filter_state: applied` on all five relay pages, span 07-07 → 08-07) — cap_R min 2.9837 · median 4.2924 · max 7.5639 against arm 1.0, so **30/30 clear at a tightest margin of 2.98×**, and `pkg-29261c70cb4e41ba` (trade 4511) closed `giveback_stop`, making this the only row in the registry graded on the lever *firing* rather than on a ceiling computation. **TWO rows still carry `verdict: unmeasured`** (`trend_donchian_sol_4h`, `scha_trend_long_1d`) — a live *basis* in a sub-object is not a graded verdict, and an intermediate draft that claimed the field was cleared was false. ⚠️ **The operative ceiling is the VENUE TP cap `0.099/(risk_per_unit/entry)` — the value that reaches `kwargs["takeProfit"]` — NOT `exit_plan_state.targets[].reach_r`**, which is `min(cap_R, 5.0)` from the **observe-only** ExitPlan ladder that is recorded and never placed (`src/units/accounts/execute.py:391/:703/:1467/:1875`; the sole exit-plan reference there is the soak at `:630-648`, whose comment says nothing reads it back). Settled by reading the placement path after **two prior revisions asserted opposite answers**, the second of which merged (#9785) and was corrected in #9846; reading the clamped field would grade a blanket false `inert` on the three arms above 5.0R (`trend_donchian` 6.49, `sol_4h` 5.57, `gld` 5.06). No landed verdict was wrong — `min(x,5) >= arm` iff `x >= arm` when `arm < 5`. **P4 SHIPPED AND RUN 2026-08-17** ([`S-M31-P4-MFE-PARITY-2026-08-17.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-M31-P4-MFE-PARITY-2026-08-17.md), `scripts/research/m31_mfe_parity.py`) — **two checks, deliberately separate because they need different denominators.** **Check A (ceiling — per ROW, gradeable at n=1):** a live trade's `peak_r` must not exceed its own `cap_r`. Measured over the whole live table: **14/14 `within_cap`, 0 breaches, 0 ungradeable** — peak as a share of that row's own ceiling runs `xrp_pullback_2h` **87.1%** · `ada_pullback_2h` 73.3% · `trend_donchian_ada_4h` 60.6% · the other 11 below 32%. That is the **first live MFE-vs-ceiling measurement ever taken**, and it is a real negative with a stated denominator. **Check B (distribution — needs soak depth): ABSTAINS, and the abstention IS the result.** The lifecycle join was done, not assumed (open set `filter_state: applied`, `total: 27`), giving **14 telemetry rows = 13 OPEN + 1 CLOSED** (trade 4697, `trend_donchian_sol_4h`) — so the live final-MFE population is **n=1 fleet-wide** against a floor of 8. P4's own design gate is *"needs P2 soak depth"*; that gate is **real and not yet met**, and a verdict off this population would be the exact sin the tool exists to prevent. **The harness half is missing SEPARATELY:** the committed corpus carries **no per-trade `mfe_r`** (key census over all 1,376 rows — cell aggregates only), so Check B needs a sweep re-run with `--emit-trades` as well as live depth (`PB-20260817-NO-COMMITTED-PER-TRADE-HARNESS-MFE`). ⚠️ **P4 found P2's blocking gap: `docs/design/position-telemetry-DESIGN.md` § 7.2 recommends "live row + a terminal snapshot written at close" and only the live row shipped** — `write_record` is an UPSERT, both callers are open-position monitors, there is no close path, and the table carries no `status`/`closed_at`/terminal marker, so a closed row is byte-shaped like an open one (the collapsed-state class, in a table shipped *after* that rule). Filed **`PB-20260817-TELEMETRY-HAS-NO-TERMINAL-SNAPSHOT`** (Tier-2 — it touches the live close path). Until it lands, every live MFE is a **lower bound** truncated at the last exit pass, and the checker refuses to grade without the `trades` join. CI registers **`mfe-parity-instrument-guard`** over the **self-test only** (10/10, asserting the probe still flags planted breaches/divergences and still abstains rather than passing) — deliberately **not** over the parity run, which would green on `harness_absent`/`live_no_final_rows`, a pass that checked nothing. Two corroborations fell out: `qqq_trend_long_1d`'s `inert` verdict is confirmed on a fresh row (live `cap_r 2.1258` vs declared `arm_r 3.56` = **167% of cap**), and `trend_donchian_sol_4h` — measured **0/16 over a complete population** — now has a **17th row where the arm IS below the cap** (`cap_r 5.8294` vs `arm_r 5.57`), nothing decided, but a concrete argument that "complete" is only complete up to its timestamp. **P3 READERS DONE 2026-08-17** ([`S-M31-P3-READERS-2026-08-17.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-M31-P3-READERS-2026-08-17.md)) — `GET /api/diag/position_telemetry` + an **`r` block on `/api/bot/positions`**, both resolved through one owner (`src.runtime.position_telemetry`) so the two surfaces cannot drift. They add the three things the TABLE CANNOT SAY: **`lifecycle`** (four states — the table has no status column, so a closed row is byte-shaped like an open one and the only in-table hint, a staler `updated_at`, is not a signal), **`peak_pct_of_cap`** (how close it EVER got, vs the stored `pct_of_cap`'s where-it-is-now), and **`arm_reach`** (is the declared arm reachable under THIS row's ceiling). The reader independently reproduces the hand audit: 13 open + 1 closed, **0 peaks above cap**, and **2 of 3 declared arms `unreachable`** (`xrp_pullback_2h` 4.49 vs 3.9233; `qqq_trend_long_1d` 3.56 vs 2.1258). This closes P3's own failure signal — *"rows accruing a month with no consumer"* — at **one day**, rather than the month the `exit_price_source` shape took. **P5 is PROPOSED, NOT SHIPPED, and the gate is measured NOT MET** ([`m31-p5-telemetry-reading-lever-PROPOSAL.md`](docs/design/m31-p5-telemetry-reading-lever-PROPOSAL.md)): P4 Check B abstains at **n=1** final row fleet-wide, the M20 levers have fired **13 times ever** against 1,142 closed trades, and the harness per-trade `mfe_r` is committed nowhere — so a P5 lever would be tuned on a harness whose agreement with the live book has never been checked, which is the exact defect family M31 exists to close. The candidate is named (an `rr_from_here` floor — the milestone's own motivating question, **0.71** on the XRP trade) with **five falsifiable preconditions**, so it is not re-derived. **PRECONDITION 1 CLOSED 2026-08-17** ([`S-M31-TERMINAL-STAMP-2026-08-17.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-M31-TERMINAL-STAMP-2026-08-17.md)) — the Tier-2 terminal-snapshot writer shipped (operator-approved in-conversation), closing `PB-20260817-TELEMETRY-HAS-NO-TERMINAL-SNAPSHOT`: `Database.update_trade` stamps `terminal_state='final'` + `terminal_at` on close, in its own guard so a telemetry write failure can neither propagate into nor be skipped by the trader close path. It **never overwrites an existing stamp** (a re-close must not move `terminal_at` forward — first observation is the honest one) and **never INSERTS a row** (manufacturing one at close would fabricate a trajectory that was never measured). Readers publish a four-state **`finality_source`** — `stamped` / `derived_join` / `not_final` / `unknown` — so a row predating the writer can never read as one the hook stamped; registered with `collapsed-state-guard` per the backlog item's own resolution criteria, and **mutation-tested** (collapsing `derived_join` into `stamped` makes the guard fire). ⚠️ **This NARROWS the `peak_r` lower bound, it does not eliminate it** — the stamp records when we observed finality, not a re-measured peak, so `peak_r_is_lower_bound` stays true on stamped rows too. **P5 remains withheld: precondition 2 (P4 Check B returning `compared`) is a DATA-ACCRUAL problem the writer does not solve** — it makes future closes gradeable without a join, but the fleet-wide final population is still n=1 today and the harness per-trade `mfe_r` is still committed nowhere. **M31 is therefore COMPLETE at P1–P4 with P5 deliberately withheld.** ⚠️ **Id note:** this M31 is the *position-telemetry* milestone; **"M31 Track B" elsewhere in this file** (options-skew, lines under M28) is a **signal-family label inside the M28 research program**, not this milestone. Only one milestone ROW uses M31 — the collision is in the label namespace only. **Next work + its falsifiers are written down, not left open-ended** (operator-directed 2026-08-16, *"this can't just end open ended, and then we don't do anything for two months and then get surprised"*): [`m20-m31-operator-decisions-2026-08-16.md` § 8](docs/claude/m20-m31-operator-decisions-2026-08-16.md) gives every open item an expectation, a when-we-would-know, and **the signal that says it is not working**. The load-bearing number behind the ranking: the M20 exit levers have fired **13 times ever** (`stale_stop` 10 · `exit_head` 2 · `giveback_stop` 1, that one on paper) against **1,142 closed trades** — so the live journal cannot grade them at n=2/n=1 and *waiting will not fix that*, which is why **P3 counterfactual readers rank above more sweeping**. §8's own failure signal for P3: rows accruing a month with no consumer is the `exit_price_source` failure (written in 12 files, branched on in one) repeating. **PRECONDITION 2's HARNESS HALF — MECHANISM SHIPPED 2026-08-17, ARTIFACT STILL ABSENT** ([`S-M31-HARNESS-MFE-DIST-2026-08-17.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-M31-HARNESS-MFE-DIST-2026-08-17.md); `PB-20260817-NO-COMMITTED-PER-TRADE-HARNESS-MFE` stays **OPEN**, and the distinction is the finding). Check B needs BOTH a live final-MFE population AND a harness `mfe_r` distribution. The live half is soak depth nobody can hurry; the second was missing for a duller reason — never committed — so a session waiting only on live depth would have reached the floor and THEN found the other half absent. Re-verified rather than inherited: a key census over **all 1,376** corpus rows finds **zero** keys containing `mfe`. Shipped `scripts/research/m31_harness_mfe_dist.py` (emit rows -> a small committed per-leg record: percentiles + `n`, never per-trade volume) + `m31_mfe_parity --harness-dist` to consume it, mutually exclusive with `--harness-emit` so provenance is never a function of argument order. With a committed artifact the **`tp_cap_pct` gate becomes PER-LEG** (one uncapped leg must neither condemn nor be excused by its neighbours) and `harness_source`/`harness_symbol`/`harness_timeframe` reach every record. Percentiles are IMPORTED from `m31_mfe_parity._pct` — pinned by a test that fails even against a byte-identical local copy. ⚠️ **The artifact is NOT generated here, deliberately:** `data/*.csv` is gitignored (measured — `resolve_data` returns `(None, False, None)` for all of SOLUSDT/4h, XRPUSDT/2h, BTCUSDT/1h, ADAUSDT/2h, QQQ/1d), and the only committed candles are **BTCUSDT 1-MINUTE** where the 9.9% cap lands at **~37R** against live legs at cap_R **2.13–5.83**. A fixture-derived distribution would be a wrong-regime artifact under the exact name Check B reads — M31's own defect class, authored by us and versioned — so the aggregator REFUSES an uncapped sweep and REQUIRES `--symbol`/`--timeframe`. End-to-end verified on a real capped sweep (144 emit rows, 144/144 carrying `mfe_r`, parity returned `compared`/`committed_dist`); every refusal mutation-tested — and **one mutation exposed a defect in the test itself** (the uncapped-refusal case passed with the guard DELETED, because its nonexistent `--emit` path returned the same exit code: a test asserting an exit code a different failure also produces), rewritten against a real file with a positive control ahead of it. **Remaining:** a capped trainer-side sweep per telemetry-writing leg (donchian/pullback only — no other leg writes telemetry) with `--emit-trades`. **P5 stays Tier-3 and withheld.** **PRECONDITION 3 SPLIT AND ITS *IMPLEMENT* HALF CLOSED 2026-08-17** ([`S-M31-P5-RR-FLOOR-HARNESS-2026-08-17.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-M31-P5-RR-FLOOR-HARNESS-2026-08-17.md), closing `PB-20260817-RR-FROM-HERE-LEVER-ABSENT-FROM-HARNESS`) — precondition 3 asked for a walk-forward on a lever **the harness could not express**, which is a claim about backtest evidence no artifact could produce, so it was always two steps. **3a DONE:** `scripts/backtest_trend.py` gains `--rr-floor` (default off, byte-identical, registered in `ALL_LEVERS_AT_DEFAULT` so the per-lever no-op guard covers it). It is **not a second derivation** — `position_telemetry.r_distances` was extracted as the ONE definition of `(r_to_stop, r_to_target, rr_from_here)`, `build_record` now routes through it and the harness IMPORTS it (pinned by object identity **plus** a source check that the live writer still calls it); the live mapping was read at the call site, not assumed — `stop` is the CURRENT TRAILED stop (`trail`), `target` the VENUE-CAPPED tp (`tp_price`). ⚠️ **The lever is structurally unmeasurable without a capped TP** (no `tp_price` ⇒ no `r_to_target` ⇒ it cannot fire), and such a run returns exactly-zero deltas **byte-identical to a measured no-op** — the `BL-20260817-A-SHIPPED-LEVER-RE-SWEPT-AGAINST-ITSELF-READS-AS-A-MEASURED-NO-OP` shape a concurrent session filed hours earlier. Handled twice: three-state `rr_floor_state` (`off`/`measurable`/`unmeasurable_no_tp_cap`) registered with `collapsed-state-guard`, **and** `main()` refuses the combination outright (exit 2). New `rr_min_p10/median/p90` says which floors are reachable *before* a sweep spends a fold. POSITIVE CONTROL (floors 10/25/40/100 → **1/50/176/315** fires, monotone) proves the lever is not wired to nothing. **3b NOT DONE** (`PB-20260817-RR-FLOOR-UNMEASURED-ON-LIVE-REGIME-DATA`): the repo's only candle file is BTCUSDT **1-MINUTE** (5,000 bars, median `(high−low)/close` **0.101%**), where the 9.9% venue cap sits at `tp_r_effective_median` **36.73R** and `rr_min` runs p10 **21.16** / median **35.88** / p90 **58.36** with **zero** `take_profit` exits — against LIVE cap_R **2.13–5.83** and the motivating XRP trade's `rr_from_here` **0.71**. An order of magnitude out, so **no edge verdict is offered from that fixture** (net_total_r does fall −82.65 → −150.25 across the floors, but over a net-negative book at absurd floors in the wrong vol regime — quoting that as "the lever loses" would be this milestone's own defect class). Scope note: a limitation of the FIXTURE, **not** of the recorded sweep corpus — `m20_fleet_exit_sweep.py` resolves per-`(symbol, timeframe)` files from `--data-dir` and `backtest_candles.csv` does not parse as `(sym, tf)` at all. 3b needs per-leg 1h–1d candles, graded with `m20_wf_effective.py` so inert folds are excluded. **P5 remains Tier-3 and withheld**; precondition 2 (final population n=1 fleet-wide) is still the binding one and no code change touches it. |
| **M36** | pm-sprint (research/integration) | **Consolidation & Integration** (operator-directed 2026-07-27) — **before opening new research frontiers, go DEEPER on the open M25→M30 threads and INTEGRATE them so the system works as one picture.** Four tracks: **(A)** advance **M26** regime-transition from observe-only toward a *deployable role* (P2 soak → P3 walk-forward arms vs `hold`, per-strategy-class). **(B)** broaden **M27** to **scalp AND chop** expansion (chop-regime strategies — the regime where M26's defense lives), and **fit already-passing cells into the EXISTING portfolio with NO new integration** — route **GLD 1h** (M27 STRONG PASS) through an existing **Alpaca** account. **(C)** **MERGE M28⊕M29** into one macro-intelligence program (M29 scenario distributions condition M28 thesis conviction/sizing via `c_macro`) AND add a **positioning/crowding overlay** — model *how & when other traders trade the move* (already-priced-in / overshoot / crowding) and feed it into thesis ENTRY + EXIT STRUCTURE (e.g. move an exit up when a week-horizon thesis prices in early). **(D)** make **M30 the integration backbone** — every panel/feature/result it produces feeds the macro sleeves (where relevant) and **especially the M16 conviction master model** (`conviction`/`conviction-meta-v1`), trained on ALL workstreams + their results so it has the fullest picture. **Consolidate-before-expand:** new input classes (M31 Track B options-skew, etc.) wait. Full plan: [§ M36 — Consolidation & Integration](#m36--consolidation--integration-research-program). Anchor `MB-20260727-M36-CONSOLIDATION-INTEGRATION`. Tier-1 research/observe-only throughout (a discovered edge goes live only via the existing net-of-cost walk-forward gate + Tier-3 approval). | 🔄 **IN PROGRESS** 2026-07-27 — scoped from the operator directive; composes the open threads M16/M26/M27/M28/M29/M30 rather than opening a new frontier. **Track C** (macro merge + crowding overlay): C0 design + C1–C3 pure conditioners shipped (#7750). **Track D** (M30 backbone): tooling merged (#7752 — `backtest_system` portfolio adapter + per-regime-cell C2b driver); the M30→M16 conviction backtest-augmentation wiring + design of record (#7756); the full-647k exit-timing verdict (ledger Study 8 — thin regime-conditioned entry edge, exits null), the off-trainer `research-panel-build` infra (#7764/#7771, live-verified), and the merge-slot enforcement guard (#7768) all landed. **M16 #2 backtest-augmentation A/B ran 2026-07-27 → NEGATIVE** — `conviction-meta-v1-bt` ≤ live-only `conviction-meta-v1` on the live holdout (both sub-baseline, no edge); stays candidate/observe-only, no Tier-3 promotion (#7783). |
| **M37** | pm-sprint (infra/tooling) | **Delegated LLM burst worker + cloud-state verification** (operator-directed 2026-08-18) — two tracks that turned out to share a root. **(A) Burst worker:** a ~free LLM worker Claude drives itself for bounded coding/research subtasks. The design reframe is that the lifecycle machinery a "burst worker" seems to need (start → health-check → idle timeout → stop) only exists if you assume a long-lived ENDPOINT; for bursty delegation the unit is one bounded SUBTASK, so **a GitHub Actions job IS the worker** — nothing to stop, nothing to leak, $0 on public-repo runners. Scope is enforced by a default-deny path guard (`scripts/llm/scope_guard.py`), not by convention, and the result envelope is three-state (`completed`/`failed`/`not_attempted`) so an empty answer can never read as "found nothing". **(B) Cloud verification:** `scripts/ops/oci_inventory.py` diffs the DOCUMENTED VM topology against the actual cloud — built because the canonical docs described it in prose and nothing ever checked, a gap that had already cost two months of a silently-dead `DIAG_BASE_URL`. Design: [`llm-burst-worker-DESIGN.md`](docs/design/llm-burst-worker-DESIGN.md). Anchor `MB-20260818-M37-DELEGATED-WORKER`. Tier-1 throughout — no `src/`, no `config/`, no order path, nothing running on either VM. | ✅ **BOTH TRACKS SHIPPED AND VERIFIED 2026-08-18** ([`S-LLM-BURST-WORKER-2026-08-18.md`](docs/sprint-logs/S-LLM-BURST-WORKER-2026-08-18.md); #9936, #9945, #9948, #9953). **Backend is Gemini 3.6 Flash** via Google's OpenAI-compat shim — chosen after Cerebras was **measured** to return `402 payment_required` (the widely-quoted "1M tokens/day free" came from third-party blogs, not Cerebras docs, and does not hold on this account), and because `GEMINI_API_KEY` was already in production use here, so it is *proven* free-tier rather than assumed. **Delegation pays, at a stated denominator: 17 of 19 claims valid across 5 completed tasks (~89%)** — but that is an existence proof, NOT a hit rate, and every task reviewed code this session had just written, which is the easiest possible case. **The tool found real defects in its own authors' work**, including two SILENT-LOSS bugs: a global `concurrency` group that dropped 2 of 4 fanned-out tasks with no error anywhere, and a name-keyed OCI diff that swallowed duplicate `display_name`s while reporting CLEAN. **Track B's answer: the repo is ACCURATE** — 3 × `match`, 0 drift (run 32131324874); 4 OCPU / 24 GB, exactly what `CLAUDE.md` claims. That is precisely the legacy/PAYG Ampere ceiling and DOUBLE the 2/12 Always Free ceiling imposed 2026-06-15; nothing was shut down and the operator reports a card on file with no charges, consistent with an upgraded tenancy — **the tool records that evidence and deliberately does not assert the account type**, which is console-only. Operational consequence, firm regardless: **zero Ampere headroom**, independently confirming no LLM worker could ever have lived on this tenancy. **DISCOVERABILITY WAS THE MISSING PIECE, AND IT IS NOW CLOSED** (operator-caught 2026-08-18): both capabilities shipped OUTSIDE `.claude/skills/`, which the SessionStart hook enumerates as *the* catalog under the binding skill-first-lookup rule — so a fresh session would never have found either. That is the written-and-never-read failure class, reproduced while building a tool to catch it. Closed by: a new **`llm-delegate` skill** (when delegation pays and when it does NOT, the scope guard as a security decision, the three-state envelope, the absence-of-evidence failure mode, the reasoning-token budget), an **`oci-inventory` section in `vm-ops`** + a pointer in **`vm-migration`** (whose own hard lesson is that the environment contract breaks), and **Mode D in `delegate-work`** so the in-house delegation modes cross-reference the external one. **NEXT, with falsifiers written down rather than left open-ended** (per the operator directive recorded under M31): **(1) point the delegate at real backlog items, not its own source** — *expectation:* precision holds on unfamiliar subsystems; *failure signal:* precision drops materially, or verification starts costing more than doing the work, in which case the tool is a curiosity and should be said so plainly. **Grade those against their OWN denominator — do not pool them with the self-review numbers.** **(2) OPTIONAL, not the default:** `oci-inventory` is an on-demand check a session runs when touching topology (that is why it lives in `vm-ops`), not a recurring job. A periodic `--fail-on-drift` run is *available* if topology starts drifting unnoticed — but if it is ever added, *failure signal:* it can never go red, i.e. a green check that checked nothing, so it MUST be shown to fail on an induced drift before it is trusted. **(3)** the `oci-inventory` issue-label path is UNEXERCISED (dispatch-only so far) and the label may not exist. Deferred indefinitely: a local-GGUF backend — no privacy driver exists and the hosted path is cheaper AND better. ⚠️ **THAT PREMISE IS FALSIFIED (2026-08-23): a privacy driver DID exist and we asserted it did not.** The operator's position — that these flows should not send data to an outside service — was never asked for; "no privacy driver exists" was a claim about THEIR requirements made on our own authority. It surfaced when the prop screenshot reader was found sending broker balance, equity, account number and open positions to Anthropic and Google. Phase 3 is no longer deferred; it is gated work with a stated driver. `BL-20260823-PROP-SCREENSHOT-SENDS-LIVE-ACCOUNT-DATA-TO-HOSTED-MODELS`. ✅ **BOTH NEXT-STEPS EXECUTED 2026-08-20** ([`S-LLM-BURST-WORKER-SCHEDULING-2026-08-20`](docs/sprint-logs/S-LLM-BURST-WORKER-SCHEDULING-2026-08-20.md); PR #10031). **(1) `--fail-on-drift` is scheduled, and the falsifier was satisfied BEFORE the cron was added, not after.** Both arms against the real cloud: run **32351336623** with the expectations file deliberately wrong on a throwaway branch returned `drift 1 · match 1 · missing 1 · undeclared 1`, **exit 1**; run **32351503893** with the same flag on unmodified `main` returned `3 match`, **exit 0**. The lone `match` in the red arm is the **denominator assertion** — it proves the tool read the cloud rather than failing before the inventory ran. Drift was induced on the DECLARED side deliberately (`diff()` compares symmetrically; terminating a live VM to test a checker is not a trade this repo makes). ⚠️ **THE CONTROL FOUND A REAL DEFECT, which is the whole argument for running it:** the runner's default shell is `bash -e {0}` and the job adds `set -o pipefail`, so on the drift path the failing `python3 … | tee` pipeline aborted the script BEFORE the rc capture — measured `RC: 0` on green and `RC:` **EMPTY** on red, so the posted report read **"exit `unknown`" on precisely the runs that found something**. Invisible until then because the drift path had never once run end-to-end. The verdict is now four states derived from the REPORT, not the exit code (`clean` / `drift` / `not_declared` / `could_not_check`), and `could_not_check` neither reads as clean nor opens a "topology drifted" issue. The **issue-label path also fired for the first time** (run **32351518408**, `event=issues`, not `skipped`). ✅ **THE SCHEDULE TRIGGER IS NOW PROVEN, SAME DAY** (this row previously read *"the SCHEDULE itself has never fired — inferred, not verified"*; corrected on measurement, not on the merge). A dispatch CANNOT reach that disjunct by construction — `inputs.fail_on_drift` carries `default: "false"` and is therefore never empty — so two **one-off probe crons** were merged and removed the same day (#10037 in, #10043 out, day-of-month+month scoped so a forgotten removal degrades to one stray run in August 2027, never a second weekly cron). Run **32362843960**, `event=schedule`, conclusion **success**: the job **RAN** (not `skipped`), verdict **`clean`**, Auto-resolve success, Telegram / drift-issue / fail-the-run all correctly skipped — and it is the first live exercise of the `EVENT_NAME = schedule` branch that defaults the flag ON, so the enforcing default is confirmed in production rather than inferred. The pass condition was posted to board #6927 **before** the result so it could not be rewritten to fit. **n = 1, deliberately not rounded up:** the second window produced no run, but that is almost certainly the removal landing at 11:29Z inside the lag budget, not GitHub dropping it — so this reads *"the trigger works"*, not *"the trigger is reliable"*; Monday supplies the second point for free. ⚠️ **The reusable finding: GitHub scheduling lag is real** — the 11:00Z window fired at **11:14:30Z, 14.5 minutes late**. The two windows were a hedge against GitHub dropping one; what they actually bought was protection against a READER concluding at 11:05Z that the gate was broken. A late scheduled run is not a missed one — recorded at the cron itself and on the backlog row so it is not re-derived as a defect on the first quiet Monday. **(2) The delegate was pointed at 5 real open backlog items on code the session did not write, and the two columns disagree — which IS the finding: substantive claims 20/20, line citations 0/20.** **Not pooled with the pilot's 17/19**, which was graded on the delegate's own freshly-written source. Substance held on unfamiliar code, including a planted trap (`exchange_flat_reconciled` does NOT match a `startswith("reconciler")` test — it said so unprompted) and two correct refusals to assert about files it had not been given, the documented weak mode. But every line number was wrong (off by 3, 24, ~214) while every quoted snippet was verbatim correct, and **the cause was OURS**: `build_prompt` sent raw content, so "cite the line number" could only be answered by counting. Fixed (`number_lines`, tab-separated, + a SYSTEM_PROMPT clause), plant-proven. **The pilot never saw it because its prompts asked for the "exact expression" rather than a line number** — the prompt shape hid a defect that was always there. **Post-fix citation accuracy is UNMEASURED**; re-grading the same five task ids is the cheap next step, and if they still miss, the missing numbers were not the cause and the fix is cosmetic. **That gap, and every other one this milestone left open, now carries a TIMER rather than prose in a sprint log** (operator-directed 2026-08-20; `snoozed_until` + `trigger_condition` + `what_to_check`, each naming what would FALSIFY it rather than confirm it): `BL-20260820-DELEGATE-LINE-CITATIONS-UNMEASURED-AFTER-NUMBERING-FIX` (2026-08-27, and grade the NUMBER independently of the quoted expression — checking only the quote is what hid the defect the first time) · `BL-20260820-EXCHANGE-FILLS-IB-DELEGATE-FINDINGS-NOT-DATA-VERIFIED` (2026-09-03, put an incidence figure with a stated denominator on each of the four) · `BL-20260820-OCI-INVENTORY-ISSUE-PATH-CANNOT-REQUEST-FAIL-ON-DRIFT` (2026-09-01, the label path always resolves the flag false and nothing says so at trigger time). A stated limitation nobody re-reads decays into an assumed fact, which is the same class as the deduped-but-not-deduped backlog rows this session was sent to re-check. Four `exchange_fills_ib.py` findings filed (each re-derived by grep, since its own citations do not land), worst being the `cumQty` fallback that would double-count partial fills in the exchange-truth store — filed **latent**, because whether `shares` is ever absent live is unmeasured. |
| **M38** | pm-sprint (infra/privacy) | **Local vision backend — the prop screenshot reader without an outside service** (operator-directed 2026-08-23: *"we still need to build out this LLM … the Anthropic credits are not relevant here at all. We cancel that out … We need to make sure that the screenshot parser is actually going to work on the local LLM, on the cloud, and not waiting for credits from external supports that aren't relevant."*). **This is NOT "add a GGUF backend to the burst worker", and that matters — the obvious plan is unavailable and would be WORSE than what it replaces.** Verified 2026-08-23: the repo is **PUBLIC** (`"private": false` from the GitHub API), and `llm-delegate` prints its result envelope **to the run log**, so routing a broker screenshot through it would put account balance, equity, the account number and open positions into a **world-readable** run log — published rather than merely transmitted. It is also **text-only** (zero matches for `image`, `vision`, `inline_data` or `base64` across `scripts/llm/*.py`). A self-hosted runner does not rescue it (GitHub's own guidance forbids them on public repos — a fork PR runs arbitrary code). **So this is a separate service, and it supersedes `llm-burst-worker-DESIGN.md`'s Phase-3 line for this flow.** Proposed host: the **trainer VM** (1 OCPU / 6 GB Ampere), which is the only box that is simultaneously ours, already allocated (**zero Ampere headroom** — `oci_inventory` run 32131324874, 3 × match, 4 OCPU / 24 GB full) and **not the money box**; a saturation event there costs a delayed training cycle, not a trade. Shape: **one bounded process per read, destroyed on completion** — the burst worker's own "the job IS the worker" reframe applied one level down, because a resident 2–3 GB server would hold RAM away from the trainer's actual job >99% of the time for a handful of screenshots a day. **THE ACCURACY GATE IS THE WORK; THE PLUMBING IS THE EASY HALF** — the extracted balance feeds `prop_account_status` → `/api/bot/prop/status` rule-distance → the **$4,700 static-DD floor**, so a misread digit is a safety event. Measured instance, same day: typed `bal 5040 5010` vs the screenshot's **4869 / 4867**, $171 apart — a **$340** cushion versus a **$169** one. Gate: per-FIELD exact match on money numbers, **honest-null scored in its own column against its own denominator and never pooled** (the M37 20/20-vs-0/20 lesson), the **hosted path as the control arm**, and a stated denominator on every figure. ⚠️ **P0 blocks everything: no labelled corpus exists** — Telegram images are downloaded into memory and discarded — and it can **never be committed** (a screenshot carries the broker account number and this repo is public), so it lives on the VM and the gate runs there, never in CI. Design: [`local-vision-backend-DESIGN.md`](docs/design/local-vision-backend-DESIGN.md). Tracking: `BL-20260823-PROP-SCREENSHOT-SENDS-LIVE-ACCOUNT-DATA-TO-HOSTED-MODELS`. Tier 1 for P0–P2 (fixtures, harness, measurement), Tier 2 for P3 (a new service the live VM calls) and P4 (flipping the default). | ⛔ **SCREENSHOT DRIVER WITHDRAWN BY THE OPERATOR 2026-08-23 — CLOSED, not deferred; do NOT start P0.** Operator, after reading the constraint analysis: *"perhaps it's not worth pursuing. We should still build it just not for the screenshots … we'll not do the screenshots via Telegram. I'll just keep on handing them to you in a cloud chat."* **The driver was withdrawn once the cost was measured, which is the correct outcome of a feasibility study rather than a failure of one** — and note what it did NOT cost: **the egress this milestone was opened to close is closed and stays closed, without a local model ever being built.** `PROP_SCREENSHOT_BACKEND` keeps its `local` default, which now means *"deliberately not served"* rather than *"not built yet"*. **The replacement path already works and has been exercised**: the operator hands the screenshot to a Claude session, which files the report through the same `prop_report.ingest_report` chokepoint via the `prop-report` relay — exactly how **fill id 34** reached the journal on 2026-08-23 while the reader was broken. The typed grammar is unchanged and remains the fastest path. Stated once without editorialising: a screenshot handed to a Claude session is also handled outside the trading system; what the operator is buying is **deliberate, per-image, human-directed** handling instead of an automated pipeline shipping account data on every photo with nothing declaring that it did — which was the actual complaint. **WHAT SURVIVES:** the operator also said *"we should still build it, just not for the screenshots"*, so the measured constraints above — in particular that the **public-repo burst worker cannot carry ANY sensitive payload** — stand for whatever that turns out to be, and are the expensive part of the work. Re-read that finding before anyone proposes routing sensitive data through `llm-delegate` again. The design doc is preserved as **a record, not a plan**. Original planning detail follows. **P0 was never started.** **What is true TODAY, stated plainly rather than buried: the screenshot reader does not read screenshots.** `PROP_SCREENSHOT_BACKEND` defaults to `local`, which **refuses** rather than silently calling a hosted model; the manual bridge falls back to the typed grammar, which works and is unaffected (`bal 5040 5010`, `close ETHUSD 2950 +80 tp`) and is also the only path whose numbers never passed through a model. `local` / `external` / `off` are three states never collapsed — *"should work, not built yet"* and *"we chose not to"* are different statements — and an unparseable value fails closed. **Two levers, both the operator's:** leave it at `local` (nothing leaves the system) or set `external` (today's hosted behaviour, deliberately and on the record; note the Anthropic key is out of credit so `external` currently resolves to the Gemini **free tier**, whose data-use terms are a different contract from the paid tier — check them first; neither the design nor this row asserts what they say). **Each phase carries a FALSIFIER, not an intent** (the standing M31 directive): P0 falsifies if the corpus holds too few distinct LAYOUTS to state a denominator over; P1 if the harness cannot reproduce the known 4869-vs-5040 conflict (a scorer that cannot show the one error on record is not measuring the thing); **P2 if no local candidate reaches the hosted control's per-field accuracy at acceptable latency on 1 OCPU — in which case the honest outcome is that the feature stays typed-report-only, and saying so is a result, not a failure to try**; P3 if a read contends with a training cycle in normal operation; P4 if live field accuracy diverges from P2's fixture figure. **Deterministic OCR (Tesseract) is kept as a live alternative, not dismissed** — it loses on layout variance and on honest-null, but it is measured on the same fixtures in P2 and wins if it clears the gate; choosing the VLM before measuring would be picking the fancier tool, not the better one. **Unmeasured and stated as such:** trainer private-subnet reachability from live, VLM throughput on 1 Ampere OCPU (**no token/s figure appears anywhere in the design on purpose** — the M37 Cerebras lesson is that a widely-quoted outside number is not evidence about this system), the trainer's own duty cycle, and whether any small local VLM can read a dense DXtrade table at all (the failure mode is a plausible wrong digit, not a refusal). ⚠️ **Billing is NOT a blocker on this milestone and must not be re-quoted as one** — an earlier session reported the live `credit balance is too low` 400 as *"operator action, add credits"*, which was wrong: paying to keep broker screenshots flowing to a third party is not a fix for a path that should not have been live. `BL-20260823-PROP-SCREENSHOT-SENDS-LIVE-ACCOUNT-DATA-TO-HOSTED-MODELS`. |
| **M39** | pm-sprint (integrity/architecture) | **Measurement integrity & cross-cutting invariant enforcement** (operator-directed 2026-08-24 — *"if we see that trades aren't closing properly, or that there are bugs that are not really resolving themselves over time because we're just putting on band-aids and we need a bigger structural fix, those are also things you should be looking for"*). The first milestone opened by the new `review_coverage.structural_health` pass, which measures the RUNNING SYSTEM rather than the backlog. **Two tracks, one root — a cross-cutting concern implemented AT the main pipeline instead of at an interface every order path must satisfy, so any path built around the pipeline silently inherits none of it.** **(A) The decided-exit path is the unmeasured one.** Measured over all closed non-backtest rows with `pnl NOT NULL` (n=1151, whole history): strategy-DECIDED closes are **27.0% MEASURED** provenance vs the reconciler/janitor path's **52.0%**, and **41.8% of decided closes carry no provenance stamp at all**. By path: `vwap_cross` 67 of 102 unstamped (65.7%) — and all 102 are real money, i.e. 22.9% of the 446 real-money closed rows — plus `sl_cross` 21/96 and `tp_cross` 5/17. This inverts the intuitive reading: the path whose quality **M20 exists to improve is the path we cannot measure**, so any M20 lever result on those legs is unfalsifiable until it is fixed. **(B) The pairs sleeve is a second trading system.** `pairs_revert` 42/42 and `pairs_stop` 37/37 — **79 of 79 closes unstamped**, the only exit paths in the system with a 100% miss. It runs via `pairs_executor.run_pairs_tick` straight from `src/main.py` rather than `multi_account_execute`, the netting reconciler skips it BY DESIGN, and its execution gate lives in `config/pairs.yaml` enforced inside its own executor: three invariants, three independent misses, one cause. Scope closes with a CI guard enumerating what every order path must satisfy, so the NEXT isolated path cannot skip them silently (presence-only markers are not acceptable — the `new-table-wiring-guard` lesson). | 📋 BACKLOG — **operator-selected 2026-08-24: track (A) is the next work block**, chosen over "exit execution path" and "one divergence owner" because it is the smallest of the three AND it unblocks measuring the other two; track (B) chosen as a conformance pass with **the sleeve left running** (`bybit_1` demo-only, so in-place work carries no real-money risk). Rows filed with full measurements + resolution criteria: `BL-20260824-THE-DECIDED-EXIT-PATH-IS-THE-UNMEASURED-ONE` (A) and `BL-20260824-PAIRS-SLEEVE-IS-A-SECOND-SYSTEM-THAT-MISSES-INVARIANTS-BY-CONSTRUCTION` (B). Both Tier-2 (they change what the monitor records at close). **(A) SHIPPED 2026-08-24 (#10237, `05b06dd`) and is live on the trader** — the decided close now stamps the source it actually used, stamps `UNMEASURED_MARKER` when it had no price instead of staying silent, and no longer overwrites a more specific existing stamp (a second, laundering defect the tests caught — not the one the fix was written for). ⚠️ **DO NOT MARK (A) DONE — criterion 2 is still UNEXERCISED.** Measured post-deploy 2026-08-25 (population: the 400 newest journal rows, 219 closed, deploy ~2026-08-24T21:30Z): **4** non-pairs closes, every one carrying a source (`bybit_closed_pnl` ×2, `exchange_fill`, `candle_at_close`) — but all four *had* a real exit price, so none reached the `unmeasured` branch the fix added. No regression observed; **no exercise either**. Verification needs a decided close with NO price. **(B) not started — and its measurement is now far stronger than the argument it was filed on.** Re-measured live 2026-08-25 over that same population (119 closed `pairs_*` rows): decided pairs closes (`pairs_stop` 34 · `pairs_revert` 33 · `pairs_half_open_cleanup` 24 · `pairs_timeout` 2) are **93 of 93 provenance-silent, and all 93 carry a non-null `pnl`** — so all 93 grade `UNVERIFIED` under `classify_pnl` — against **0 of 26** for the pairs janitor path, which stamps correctly (`candle_at_close` ×23, `entry_order_avg_price_unreliable` ×3). Total separation, n=119. That is the **same inversion as (A), reproduced independently inside the isolated executor**, which is precisely the milestone's thesis: the sleeve misses the invariant **by construction, not by oversight**. |
| **M40** | pm-sprint (research infrastructure) | **Research & testing workflow — compute is solved, landing is not** (operator-directed 2026-08-27 — *"we can have more consistent, higher frequency training and backtesting, both for MLs and for strategies, with actual usable results that are recorded correctly"*). Governing doc: [`docs/research/RESEARCH-WORKFLOW-ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-27.md`](docs/research/RESEARCH-WORKFLOW-ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-27.md). **The thesis, measured: every leak found was a LANDING failure, not a throughput one** — compute is free and abundant (a 4 vCPU / 16 GB runner trains the manifest the 1-OCPU trainer OOM-quarantines), and the value was lost at the step where a result should have become readable. ⚠️ **One of the three filed instances was RETRACTED the same day and the retraction is kept as the more instructive half** (§ 0): the e35 rows had landed, and the author probed the LEVER corpus for BRACKET cells because a backlog row's `resolution_criteria` *suggested* that filename — a positive control was run **inside the same wrong file**, which controls for the probe and never for the population. | 🔄 **IN PROGRESS — six pieces, R1→R6.** **R1 one results contract** — PARTIAL: the vocabulary exists (`src/runtime/provenance.py`, `src/research/risk_basis.py`) but no single enforced row schema spans all producers. **R2 landing is part of the run** — BUILT (`scripts/ci/assert_rows_landed.py`, four states, 13 planted controls), wired into `e35-bracket-sweep.yml` and `trainer-offload-train.yml`; **18 evidence workflows still upload-and-land-nothing** (`BL-20260827-EIGHTEEN-EVIDENCE-WORKFLOWS-UPLOAD-AND-LAND-NOTHING`). **R3 close the offload loop** — TRANSPORT ✅ **2026-08-28** (#10368, `97b432a4`): a runner-trained model now commits to the tracked `ml/offload-inbox/` and `trainer_git_sync.sh` carries it; the trainer-side drain re-registers *there*, so `model_state_path.resolve()` is trainer-local **by construction** rather than by path-rewriting. ⚠️ **The drain is UNARMED on both axes** (timer enabled by nothing; `OFFLOAD_DRAIN_APPLY` must be exactly `1`) and `publish` defaults FALSE. **R4 blocking power gate** — ✅ DONE (#10365). **R5 the scheduler** — decision half DONE (#10365 routes + dispatches). **R6 decide the trainer VM's fate** — ✅ **MEASURED 2026-08-29 (see below); the inferred answer was REFUTED.** ⚠️ **TWO GATES BIND THIS LANE AND ARE THE REASON IT NEEDS A ROADMAP ROW AT ALL.** **(1) R6 is gated on R3 HOLDING, not on R3 being written.** *Holding* means a manifest trained on a runner appears in the TRAINER's registry **and its artifact loads there** — verified by loading it, not by the entry existing. Until then the trainer is still load-bearing for ML and **no residency conclusion may be drawn**; the architecture doc's own "likely answer" for R6 is labelled INFERRED and *"a hypothesis to test after R3, not a decision to take now"*. ✅ **R3 HOLDS as of 2026-08-28 — verified on the trainer by LOADING the artifact, not by the entry existing.** A `publish=true` runner dispatch (run `33150949388`) trained `btc-regime-5m-lgbm-flow-v1`; the drop merged as `cffe9010`; the trainer carried it (`model_state.json` 1,410,298 bytes present at head `7774d1c3`); the drain ran **unarmed** first (`would_ingest`, registry 95→95, nothing written); then **armed with an explicit operator OK** and reported `[ingested] btc-regime-5m-lgbm-flow-v1-offload @ 20260828T072009Z registered at stage 'candidate' and verified loadable`. **Independently re-verified**: registry 96 rows / exactly 1 offload row; `status` and `target_deployment_stage` both `candidate`; `stage_history` empty; the artifact at the registered trainer-local `model_state_path` **loads** (`booster_str`, `feature_columns`, `class_labels`) and is `PARSED EQUAL` to the inbox copy (the 609-byte delta is JSON re-serialisation, content identical). The `ict-offload-drain.timer` is installed, `enabled`+`active` (every 20 min) with `Environment=OFFLOAD_DRAIN_APPLY=1` **resolved by systemd**, and a re-run is idempotent (`[already_ingested] … no-op`, registry 96→96). ⚠️ **IT INFLUENCES NOTHING, AND THAT IS THE POINT:** `candidate` is refused by the shadow factory, and `strategies.yaml` carries **0 references** to the id. The Tier-2 moment this roadmap warns about — a runner-trained model joining the live **shadow** fleet — has **NOT** happened; that is a separate promotion. `OI-20260827-OFFLOAD-TRANSPORT-HAS-NEVER-CARRIED-A-MODEL` is CLEARED. **R6 (decide the trainer VM's fate) was therefore UNBLOCKED — and has now been RUN.** ✅ **R6 ANSWERED 2026-08-29, and the architecture doc's INFERRED answer is REFUTED** ([`R6-VM-RESIDENCY-VERDICT-2026-08-28.md`](docs/research/R6-VM-RESIDENCY-VERDICT-2026-08-28.md); two read-only trainer pulls, #10391 + #10392). The inferred residual was *"the dataset cache and 24/7 cron — leaving nothing"*. Measured, **one process genuinely requires 24/7 residency and it is not the cache**: **`ict-orderflow-capture.service`**, a continuous 2s L2 order-book capture, `active (running)` **44 days** (since 2026-07-15, PID 728, 87.4 MB, 3h53m CPU = **0.37 % of one core**), verified *writing* — newest row `2026-08-28T23:50:00Z` against a same-command `date -u` of `23:58:09`, `n_snapshots` 127–128 per 5m bar. Its consuming manifest states the constraint itself: *"The capture is **FORWARD-ONLY (no L2 history)**"* — L2 depth is not retrievable historically, so a minute not captured is gone permanently, and an ephemeral 6h-ceiling runner would take an unbackfillable gap at every handoff. ⚠️ **R3 PROVED COMPUTE IS PORTABLE; IT DID NOT PROVE ACQUISITION IS** — the model R3 registered as its own proof, `btc-regime-5m-lgbm-flow-v1`, **is the order-flow model**, trained on columns only this capture produces (*"without it the columns are 0.0 and this collapses to the v2 head"*). Reading "a runner trained a model" as "the trainer is disposable" inverts what was demonstrated. **Also uncounted by § 3: a live-serving role** — `ict-trainer-forecast` (15 min) → `ict-trainer-publish` (2 min rsync) → the live trader's per-bar regime scorer reads `fc_*`; fail-permissive (a stale artifact yields `None`, never a fabricated row), so it degrades rather than breaks. **WHY § 3 MISSED IT — the transferable half:** that table was built with `du`, and the capture's whole output is **5.7 MB = 0.02 % of the 28 G tree**, i.e. *below the resolution of the instrument used*. It answered *"what DATA is pinned"* under a heading promising *"what is pinned to the VM"* — the **UNPROVENANCED DIAGNOSTIC OUTPUT class A** substitution, one level up from code. **Residency is a property of PROCESSES and their input streams, not of stored bytes; enumerate units first, disk second.** Everything batch (`ict-trainer`, `-catchup`, `drift-retrain`, `promotion-readiness`) **is** genuinely runner-portable, and § 3's disk figures re-measure as accurate — only their scope was wrong. **VERDICT: do NOT retire the trainer on this evidence.** R6 replaced itself with a smaller, sharper question that is an **operator call, not a measurement** — *where does the order-flow capture live if the trainer does not?* It needs 87 MB and 0.37 % of a core: not **this** VM, but **an** always-on host. ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-29 — the operator decided to KEEP the trainer for the capture** (*"we can keep the training in the meantime"*), recorded as option **(c)** of `OI-20260829-ORDERFLOW-CAPTURE-HOME-UNDECIDED`'s `clears_when`: kept **as a stated decision rather than by default**, and **interim, not permanent** — reclaiming the 1 OCPU / 6 GB stays available once the capture has somewhere else to live, it simply does not happen *first*. Full record: [`R6-VM-RESIDENCY-VERDICT-2026-08-28.md` § 8](docs/research/R6-VM-RESIDENCY-VERDICT-2026-08-28.md). ⚠️ **The decision RAISES the stakes on the two findings below rather than closing them** — the box is now load-bearing *by choice*. Re-measured the same hour (relays #10422 + #10423): the capture is **alive and writing** — `datasets-out/market_microstructure/BTCUSDT/5m/v001/data.jsonl` at `17:30:00Z` against a same-command `date -u` of `17:30:17Z`, 17 s before the reference clock — and the disk is unchanged at **3.8 G free / 92 %**. ⚠️ **That path is canonical and must be copied, not guessed:** the first probe assumed `runtime_logs/orderflow/`, which does not exist, and an empty result there is **byte-identical to a dead capture**; it was separable only because a second probe carried a **positive control** (88 files modified in the same 2 h window). `journalctl` is no better — it reads `-- No entries --` on a *healthy* capture. Both findings now ride `OI-20260829-TRAINER-IS-NOW-A-DECIDED-DEPENDENCY-AND-IS-UNMONITORED` (`loud`, re-observed every 3 days), which replaces the decision row. ⚠️ **Two operational findings filed on the way, and they compound:** the trainer disk is at **92 %** (42 G/45 G, 3.9 G free, largest trees all research byproduct — `BL-20260829-TRAINER-DISK-92-PCT-THREATENS-THE-UNBACKFILLABLE-CAPTURE`), and **nothing monitors the capture at all** — zero `orderflow` references in any alerting code, absent from the diag unit allowlist, and `trainer_reachability_alert` reads the *publish* timer's mirror mtime, which advances normally with the capture dead; worse, the process catches poll errors and continues (`RequestTimeout` lines while `ActiveState=active`), so **`active (running)` is not evidence of capturing** (`BL-20260829-ORDERFLOW-CAPTURE-IS-IRREPLACEABLE-AND-UNMONITORED`). So a disk-full stall of the one irreplaceable stream would be **silent**. ⚠️ Not a fault, recorded because the exit code invites the opposite reading: `ict-drift-retrain` exiting **11** is `RETRAIN_PLAN_ONLY` (`dispatch_count=10 cli_exit=11 plan_only=1`), hourly, ~1m57s CPU each — ~47 min/day of the single core computing a plan it never executes, with 10 manifests reported due hourly and nobody acting on the report. **(2) The R5 dispatcher is deliberately NEVER FIRED.** Its daily 06:20Z cron passes no `--fire` and must not be armed until a session has read one real dry-run report against live queue contents: **none of the three spend gates** — the burst workflow's ledger preflight against the $10/month cap, its `GPU_BURST_ARMED` gate, and the dispatcher's own `--max-gpu-dispatches-per-run` — **has ever been observed holding against this dispatcher**, and a test with a stubbed `_fire` proves the counter advances while proving nothing about the ledger preflight (`OI-20260827-RESEARCH-QUEUE-DISPATCHER-NEVER-FIRED`). **Tier note:** the moment a runner-trained model first joins the live shadow fleet is **Tier-2** and takes an explicit operator OK — it is not slipped in as plumbing. **✅ 2026-08-31 — LANDING IS SOLVED END TO END, AND THE BOTTLENECK MOVED TO READING** ([`S-RESEARCH-DISPOSITION-2026-08-31`](docs/sprint-logs/S-RESEARCH-DISPOSITION-2026-08-31.md), 9 PRs). The chain was proven live for the first time: dispatch `33388490432` → sweep `33388518030` → #10576/#10577 merged, with **995 rows carrying `RQ-20260831-002`/`accruing`** where the baseline had `research_unit: None` on all 9,744. So PRODUCE · LAND · READ all work. **The fourth layer, DISPOSITION, had no write surface at all**: `research_disposition.append` was complete and well-guarded while `main` exposed only the read flags, so every one of the 75 pre-existing ledger entries was written by a hand-rolled snippet that **bypassed `append` and therefore every refusal the queue's leniency depends on** — the `exit_price_source` shape inverted, a reader with no way to write down what it read. `--record` closes it (#10589). ⚠️ **THE HONEST NEGATIVE, AND IT IS NOT WHAT IT FIRST LOOKS LIKE.** 14 units were read (42 unread → 28): 98 gated cells, **zero passes**. Read alone that says the sweep found nothing. It does not — the 28 units that **cannot be power-graded** (no `base_oos_trades`, unbackfillable because the source `report.json` files are workflow artifacts and are never committed) hold 189 gated cells and **ALL 17 passes in the corpus**, topped by `gld_pullback_1h/tp6_sm1.5_to24` at **+19.92 OOS Δnet_R on 6/6 effective folds**. **The positive results and the readable results are DISJOINT SETS and the mechanism can only read the empty one** (`BL-20260831-EVERY-PASSING-E35-CELL-IS-IN-THE-BATCH-THE-POWER-GATE-CANNOT-READ`, high). ⚠️ **Two reading traps that cost this session real time and will cost the next one more:** a pass-matcher looking for `pass`/`ok`/`promote` returns **zero** on this corpus — every passing verdict is spelled `wf_pass` or `path_b_wf_pass`; and `n_oos` is stamped on only the **gated** rows (measured 7 of 199, 3.5%), so it describes the gated subset, not the unit. **NEXT, and not started deliberately:** re-sweep those 28 legs so their passes become gradeable — a multi-hour runner job left with the operator rather than fired. Several of the 17 are sub-economic (+0.1238 ×3) and `path_b` speaks to net_R across folds and **not** to `rate_ok`, so these are leads to read, not changes to make; any bracket change stays Tier-3. Also shipped: the corpus **history sidecar** (a re-sweep supersedes on `measurement_key` and had been destroying ~955 measurements per run) and **diff-scoped `session-brief-guard`** (it was stranding branches for staleness they did not introduce). **✅ 2026-08-31 (later, session `01P2QBKN`) — THE 27 UNREADABLE LEGS ARE NOW READ, AND THE HEADLINE ABOVE IS SUPERSEDED** ([`S-E35-RESWEEP-DISPOSITION-2026-08-31`](docs/sprint-logs/S-E35-RESWEEP-DISPOSITION-2026-08-31.md)). ⚠️ **TWO NUMBERS IN THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE ARE WRONG AND WERE WRONG IN THE SAME DIRECTION — they overstated the job.** It is **27** e35 legs, not 28 (the 28th unread unit is `m20/ict_scalp_mgc_15m`, a different corpus, dispositioned `underpowered` without any sweep); and it is **not a multi-hour job** — the 2026-08-29 run of this same fleet took **42m45s** on the sharded workflow, and mine took ~40 min. The multi-hour figure predates the sharding. **The split target was the whole decision, and it was made on a measurement rather than a default:** at `split_target_oos=50` the morning batch achieved 4–50 OOS trades and **only 1 of 14 legs cleared the 49.06 floor**, so a re-sweep at 50 would have made all 27 gradeable and graded ~all of them `underpowered`, reading none of the passes. Operator approved **60**. Run `33411906178`: 5,373 rows / 27 legs / 189 gated cells / **16 passes**, and **17 of 27 legs cleared the floor**. `unread` **27 → 0**; ledger 76 → **103** dispositioned. **Outcome — 1 `actioned`, 16 `no_action_warranted`, 10 `underpowered`, and the `actioned` is the ledger's FIRST in 117 entries.** It is `gld_pullback_1h`, a **LIVE** leg at n=59: `sm1.5` +19.1188 at 5/6 effective folds, `tp6_sm1.5_to24` +17.7970 at 6/6, `tp4` +4.3415 on the **stronger Path A** gate, zero inert folds — filed as a Tier-3 proposal, `config/strategies.yaml` NOT edited off a surface scan. ⚠️ **The refusals are the more useful half:** `trend_donchian_1h` passes at power (`to96` +12.5856, Path A) but is `enabled:false`/`execution:shadow`, so **no live geometry exists to change**; `spy_pullback_1h` (LIVE) is path_b-only and **halved** across the two boundaries (+8.8415 → +4.4566), with `sm1.5_to400` reporting the identical figure (the timeout does not bind, so it is not a third pass); `avax_pullback_2h` +1.3072 carries an **inert** fold; `htf_pullback_trend_2h` +0.3861 and `trend_donchian_sol` +0.0415 are economically nothing. **11 floor-clearing legs passed nothing at all — a POWERED NEGATIVE, which is a result**, and is exactly what separates them from the 10 under-floor legs. Those 10 are **structurally thin** (4–10 OOS trades, 9 of 10 on 1d bars): no split target fixes a daily strategy that cannot produce 60 OOS trades in 1830 days, so they are data-acquisition tasks and re-running is not their remedy. ⚠️ **THE CAVEAT THAT SURVIVES THE CLOSE:** the two runs read a trailing 1830-day window **two days apart**, so they are largely the SAME data cut at a different IS/OOS boundary — their agreement is meaningful but is **NOT** out-of-sample replication. ⚠️ **Found by exercising the brand-new history sidecar for the first time:** it archived 10,547 rows of which only 5,174 are genuine supersedes — the other **5,373 are copies of the run's own rows**, because `aggregate` uploads `e35-bracket-ALL` containing every per-leg artifact and the `corpus` job's `pattern: e35-bracket-*` matches both, so each report is scanned twice. The **corpus is clean** (100% per-leg sourced); only the archive's meaning is damaged (`BL-20260831-CORPUS-JOB-SCANS-EVERY-LEG-REPORT-TWICE-SO-THE-HISTORY-SIDECAR-ARCHIVES-THE-RUNS-OWN-ROWS`). Also learned: `measurement_key` carries `split_mode|split_target_oos`, so a re-sweep at a **different target does not supersede** the prior gated rows — both coexist, nothing was destroyed, but a corpus-wide pass count now double-counts these legs. `superseded_unread` is **256**, and this session filed the row establishing that ~11% of it is demonstrably benign re-measurement residue while the rest is UNMEASURED — not benign, and not a defect either. |

**Non-negotiable rules:**

- Live trading safety > feature growth.
- No heavy training on the Oracle live VM (WS9).
- No model in live strategy logic without staged promotion +
  operator approval.
- AI output cannot bypass risk caps, broker validation, or
  mission-aware account restrictions.
- Architecture-changing code updates the architecture docs in
  the same PR.
- No auto-publishing datasets to HF (S-AI-WS3).
- No editing past `StatusEvent` entries in the registry
  (S-AI-WS4).
- No promoting to `live-approved` or `champion` without operator
  approval recorded in `--by` + `--reason` (S-AI-WS4).
- No outcome columns as features against `won` on `trade_outcomes`
  (S-AI-WS5-A).
- **No `ICT_OFFVM_BUILD_HOST=1` on the Oracle live VM**
  (S-AI-WS5-B-PART-1).

### M14 — ML Optimization Program (sprint breakdown)

> Master plan + per-session detail: [`docs/ml/optimization-roadmap.md`](docs/ml/optimization-roadmap.md).
> Each sprint is one focused session. Trainer-side tooling = **Tier-1** (autonomous);
> any new/edited `ml/configs/*.yaml` manifest or live-runtime/order-path change = **Tier-2/3**
> (operator-gated). The plan *proposes*; the operator gates every shadow→advisory flip.
> `/ml-review` reports progress against this table each run.

**Recommended execution order** (not the numeric order): **S13 first** (per-bar regime
scoring — independent, unblocks the whole regime promotion pipeline), then the **S1–S4**
discipline foundation, then **S5–S6** (the data wall), then features/regime/MLOps as
capacity allows. S1–S4 are prerequisites for trusting S5+ results.

| **S-MLOPT-S5** | 1.1 | Triple-barrier labeler → `setup_candidates` dataset family | 1 | 🔄 IN REVIEW 2026-06-03 ([log](docs/sprint-logs/S-MLOPT-S5.md)). | **S-MLOPT-S6** | 1.2 | Meta-labeling decision model (the proper setup-quality) | 1 / 3 | 🔄 IN REVIEW 2026-06-03 ([log](docs/sprint-logs/S-MLOPT-S6.md)). de Prado meta-labeling: reuses the LightGBM-regression trainer on the binary `won` target (regress→probability) + `ClassificationEvaluator`, trained on the dense `setup_candidates` (S5). **Domain-shift discipline shipped**: `setup_candidates` now appends REAL closed trades (`live_trades_db`) in the same feature space (`is_live_trade` flag), and a new **`live_holdout` split** (`ml/experiments/splitters.py`) trains on synthetic / evaluates on the REAL trades — the de-Prado-correct replacement for `setup-quality-lgbm-v2` (which lost to the baseline at n=80). Tier-1 trainer/family/splitter; the manifest (`ml/configs/setup-candidates-metalabel-v1.yaml`) is the **Tier-3 proposal** (research_only; promotion past shadow operator-gated). Draft PR — NOT auto-merged. **Trainer-VM live-holdout eval (#2697): honest NEGATIVE — on 352 real BTCUSDT trades (24.4% win rate) the model scores acc 0.670 < majority-baseline 0.756 → does NOT beat baseline → no promotion.** The leak-free real-trade eval correctly blocks a synthetic-only-good model; the synthetic↔real distribution gap (0.457 vs 0.244 win rate) points to wiring logged signals as the event source + S7/S8. |

| **S-MLOPT-S14** | 3.2 | Causal (filtered) HMM / GMM regime family | 1 / 3 | 🔄 IN REVIEW 2026-06-04 ([log](docs/sprint-logs/S-MLOPT-S14.md)). New pure-NumPy **causal Gaussian-HMM regime trainer** (`ml/trainers/causal_hmm_regime.py`: diagonal-Gaussian EM over the S9 range-vol features → emissions + soft-counted transition matrix + per-state regime-label distribution) + pure-stdlib **filtered predictor** (`ml/predictors/causal_hmm_regime.py`: forward-only `alpha_t ∝ e_k(x_t)·Σ_i alpha_{t-1}(i)·A[i,k]` — **never Viterbi/forward-backward smoothing**, so it cannot leak the future; stateful per chronological pass with an auto-reset on non-monotonic `ts` → no cross-fold leak). Research_only A/B manifest `btc-regime-1h-hmm-v1.yaml` vs the `btc-regime-1h-lgbm-v2` champion. 11 tests incl. the **causal-invariance** test (filtered posterior at t unchanged by future bars) + evaluator integration (>0.9 acc on separable synthetic regimes); ruff clean; manifest loads. **Tier-1 tooling** (trainer/predictor/tests) committed; **the manifest is a Tier-3 research_only proposal**. **Trainer-VM purged-CV A/B (#2784) — honest NEGATIVE:** the HMM loses to the LightGBM head on `macro_f1` (0.5675 vs 0.6537, −0.086) and `f1_volatile` (0.2688 vs 0.5010, −0.232) under the leak-free CV — the filtered posterior lags regime transitions (volatile recall 0.19 vs 0.64), so it under-detects the class it exists for. **Not promoted; stays research_only** (per the "illusion of regimes" success criterion); the HMM family **infra stands as reusable tooling** (like S11). The A/B also reconfirmed `btc-regime-1h-lgbm-v2`'s real purged-CV strength (macro_f1 0.654, f1_volatile 0.501) → it's the viable phase-4 detector candidate for S15. |
| **S-MLOPT-S15** | 3.3 | Regime-router phase-4 detector wiring (closes MB-20260601-002) | 2 / 3 | 🔄 IN REVIEW 2026-06-04 ([log](docs/sprint-logs/S-MLOPT-S15.md)). **Scoping found phase-4 was mis-specified:** the ADX detector + policy table key on a **trend** axis (`chop`/`transitional`/`trending`) but every registry regime model predicts a **volatility** axis (`range`/`volatile`) — so the classifier can't drop-in replace ADX. Operator chose to pursue **both** resolutions as separate tracks. **S15a (shipped):** a **trend-regime model** — new forward trend label (`ml/datasets/labeling/trend_regime.py`, Kaufman efficiency ratio → chop/transitional/trending) wired into `market_features` as `trend_regime_label` (builder_version v6→v7, default-preserving, leak-safe) + research_only manifest `btc-regime-1h-trend-lgbm-v1.yaml` (same features/dataset/split as the v2 vol head). The true ADX-detector drop-in candidate. 16 tests + 69 in the regime/market_features suite pass; ruff clean. **Stage-drift reconciled** (`baseline-regime-classifier.yaml` shadow→research_only, field-beats-comment; it collapses to majority class so it's NOT the phase-4 detector). **Trainer-VM purged-CV A/B (#2787) — POSITIVE-but-modest, leak-free:** the trend model does NOT collapse (macro_f1 0.325 vs ~0.185 majority; predicts all 3 regimes), purged≈holdout (no leakage), strongest on chop (f1 0.463), decent trending (0.348), weak transitional (0.163). A viable non-degenerate trend-axis detector candidate (the artifact that didn't exist before); stays research_only. **S15a class-weight tuning + ADX head-to-head DONE 2026-06-07** (trainer-vm-diag #2925/#2926): mild inverse-freq weights `{chop:1.0, transitional:1.45, trending:1.14}` lift transitional f1 0.167→0.276 (recall →0.286) without collapsing chop → leak-free purged-WF-CV macro_f1 **0.3403** (chop 0.393 / trans 0.276 / trend 0.353) vs **ADX-14 0.3167** (chop 0.336 / trans 0.221 / trend 0.393) — beats the ADX threshold detector overall + on 2 of 3 classes (a modest, honest win). Weights baked into the manifest (still research_only). Remaining gate to going live as the phase-4 detector: a shadow track record + backtest/shadow-PnL justification (Tier-3). **S15b (queued):** wire the existing vol classifier as a **second, observe-only** regime gate (2-D trend×vol policy) — accrues shadow evidence; enforcement waits on backtest/shadow PnL. Live phase-4 enforcement stays Tier-3 (needs a shadow track record + feature parity `MB-20260604-005`). |
| **S-MLOPT-S15b** | 3.3 | Volatility-axis regime gate (2-D trend×vol, observe-only) | 2 | 🔄 IN REVIEW 2026-06-04 ([log](docs/sprint-logs/S-MLOPT-S15b.md)). Track B of the phase-4 taxonomy fix: wires the existing vol classifier as a **second, orthogonal, observe-only** regime axis so the router logs a 2-D `trend × vol` would-gate (no enforcement) and accrues evidence for a later Tier-3 decision. New `src/runtime/regime/vol_detector.py::detect_vol_regime` — a pure detector (parallel to the ADX `detector.py`) that buckets the live `rolling_log_return_vol` against the **deployed regime head's frozen `vol_bucket` edges** (resolved once/process from the registry's shadow-stage specs, the same `regime_spec_of` source S13 uses; reuses `regime_shadow` bucketing verbatim) and **collapses to 2-class `calm`/`volatile`** (lowest bucket→calm, else volatile — operator-chosen taxonomy). **Parity guard:** only adopts edges from heads whose frozen `vol_feature_column` is `rolling_log_return_vol` (skips the yz heads so their `yang_zhang_vol` edges can't mis-place the boundary). `would_gate(..., vol_regime=)` extended to evaluate a 2-D `trend_vol` cell, **default-preserving** (byte-identical 1-D shape when no vol label). `StrategyIntent.vol_regime` + `intent_from_signal` + `_stamp_regime_on_meta` (all 11 builders) + `_shadow_regime_gate` thread/log the vol axis (`enforced:false`, both axes on each row). `config/regime_policy.yaml` `schema_version 1→2` + a documented **EMPTY** `trend_vol: {}` block (authoring `off` cells needs a vol-split of the matrix — evidence accruing now; no fabricated decisions). 31 new tests; 169 in the regime/intent/builder suites pass; ruff clean. **Observe-only / Tier-2** — no order-path reach; draft PR pending one operator OK before merge+deploy. Enforcement + the S15a trend-model follow-ups (class-weight tuning, head-to-head vs ADX-14) + feature parity (`MB-20260604-005`) remain the gates to going live. |
| **S-MLOPT-S16** | 4.1 | Drift-triggered, recency-weighted retraining (ADWIN) | 1 / 2 | 🔄 IN REVIEW 2026-06-07. New **pure-stdlib ADWIN** drift detector (`ml/shadow/adwin.py::ADWIN` + `scan_stream`) — Adaptive Windowing per Bifet & Gavaldà 2007, with the canonical Hoeffding cut `ε = √((1/2m)·ln(4w/δ))` over harmonic-mean sub-window sizes. Default `δ=0.002` (River-conservative), `min_window=10`, `max_window=10k` so a stationary head can't trip on noise and a permanently-stationary head can't grow memory. Composes with the existing window-over-window KS/PSI (`ml/shadow/drift.py`) — KS/PSI is the daily snapshot, ADWIN is the online change detector that fires retrains *between* snapshots. New **drift-retrain orchestrator** `ml/shadow/drift_retrain.py::evaluate_models` walks every deployed head (`shadow` / `advisory` / `limited_live` / `live_approved`), streams its real-time shadow scores (backfill rows excluded — synthetic timestamps would pollute the online detector), runs ADWIN, and emits one `RetrainDecision` row per head (`dispatch` / `skip_no_drift` / `skip_no_manifest` / `skip_thin_data`). New `python -m ml drift-retrain` CLI subcommand + `scripts/ops/run_drift_retrain.sh` orchestrator that fires `python -m ml train <manifest>` for each `dispatch` row — the trainer reuses whatever `sample_weight.half_life_days` the manifest already declares (the S-MLOPT-S2 recency-decay knob), so the retrain naturally down-weights the stale tail ADWIN told us to forget. **Conservative + logged**: the shell wrapper ships `RETRAIN_PLAN_ONLY=1` so the first soak only writes `runtime_logs/drift_retrain.jsonl`; flip `RETRAIN_PLAN_ONLY=0` after the operator has eyeballed a few cycles. New trainer-side `ict-drift-retrain.{service,timer}` (hourly cron, **DISABLED by default**) in `deploy/training-vm-cloud-init.yaml`. 17 new tests (`tests/ml/test_adwin.py` + `tests/ml/test_drift_retrain.py`) cover the cut bound, the stationary/step-change/min-window/cap edge cases, the manifest resolver, the watched-stage filter, the backfill exclusion, the JSONL writer, and both CLI exit codes; ruff clean. **Tier-1 trainer-side tooling — autonomous**; the systemd unit is enabled via the trainer-vm-diag relay (trainer charter — trainer-side systemd is Tier-1). **No auto-promotion past `shadow`** — trainer registers candidates, `promotion-readiness` (S18) / operator decide what clears the bar. |
| **S-MLOPT-S17** | 4.2 | Experiment tracking + train/serve feature parity | 2 | 🔄 IN REVIEW 2026-06-04 ([log](docs/sprint-logs/S-MLOPT-S17.md)). **Train/serve feature parity SHIPPED** (closes `MB-20260604-005`) — the live regime scoring row now reproduces the full `market_features` row the heads train on. `src/runtime/regime_shadow.py`: new `ohlc_from_candles` + `range_vol_estimators` (the four S9 estimators over the trailing `vol_window_n` bars, reusing `ml/datasets/volatility_estimators.py`) + `log_return_series` + `_latest_bar_hour_dow` (epoch-ms/datetime/ISO → UTC hour/dow) + `build_parity_feature_row`. `feature_row_for_predictor` rewritten to emit the superset (`rolling_log_return_vol` + parkinson/gk/rs/yz + `log_return` + `log_return_lag_1/2` + `hour_of_day`/`dayofweek`) AND **bucket `vol_bucket` against the estimator named by the head's frozen `vol_feature_column`** — so the yz heads bucket `yang_zhang_vol` (the pre-S17 bug: close-to-close vol bucketed against yz-unit edges). Both callers (`_emit_shadow_preds` signal-time, `regime_bar_scoring` per-bar) pass `candles_df=`; close-only fallback preserved for legacy callers. **Parity test uses the `MarketFeaturesBuilder` itself as oracle** — live row == builder row bit-for-bit (rel 1e-9) + a test proving the yz-bucket fix. 176 tests pass; ruff clean. **Observe-only / Tier-2** — shadow scoring feature row only, no order-path reach; draft PR pending one operator OK before merge+deploy. **Unblocks** `shadow→advisory` promotion for the strong heads (btc-regime-1h, mes-regime-1d) + the S15b vol axis on the post-deploy (parity-correct) track record (still Tier-3). Experiment-tracking half of S17 deferred. |
| **S-MLOPT-S18** | 4.3 | Champion-challenger promotion automation | 1 / 3 | 🔄 IN REVIEW 2026-06-07. New **promotion-readiness report generator** `ml/promotion/readiness_report.py` — thin orchestrator on top of `ml.promotion.stage_guard.run_stage_guard` that sweeps the whole registry (regime profile auto-applied via `thresholds_for`, OOS-edge baseline auto-selected for regime heads in `run_stage_guard` — so the yz-1h ~6/12 check and every future promotion is one CLI call), buckets the proposals into `promote` / `demote` / `hold`, and renders both a machine-readable `report.json` + an operator-readable `SUMMARY.md`. New `python -m ml promotion-readiness` CLI subcommand + `scripts/ops/run_promotion_readiness.sh` orchestrator that writes the artifact under `runtime_logs/trainer_mirror/promotion_readiness/<UTC-date>/` (so the existing `publish_trainer_mirror.sh` rsync picks it up) AND, when any model crosses the ready bar (`promotion-readiness` exits 10), pushes a one-line `pending_pings/*.json` onto the live VM via SCP — `ict-telegram-bot.service` then delivers the operator ping. Quiet days never push a ping. New trainer-side `ict-promotion-readiness.{service,timer}` (daily cron, **DISABLED by default**) in `deploy/training-vm-cloud-init.yaml`. 13 new tests (`tests/ml/test_readiness_report.py` + CLI test in `tests/ml/test_promotion_cli.py`) cover bucketing, Markdown rendering, the "missing datasets_root" warning, the quiet-vs-actionable ping message, the JSON+MD persistence, the end-to-end build_readiness_report, and the CLI's `--output-dir` write path; ruff clean. **Reports only — never auto-promotes.** The `shadow → advisory` flip stays Tier-3 (operator-gated): a `promote` proposal is an invitation, not an action. **Tier-1 trainer-side tooling — autonomous**; the systemd unit is enabled via the trainer-vm-diag relay (trainer charter — trainer-side systemd is Tier-1). |

Phase **R** (RL, GAN/diffusion, end-to-end Transformers, zero-shot foundation models)
is the **parked research shelf** — no sprint allocated; revisit only on the trigger
conditions in the master plan.

   **not** dropping signals (`gated:false`/`regime_allow_explicit`). Over the full
   window the edge is **net-positive on both accounts** → **KEEP LIVE**. Shipped a
   Tier-1 analytics guard (`exclude_reduce_leg_predicate`) so the false-alarm class
   can't recur; the only forward constraint is `bybit_2` **capital**
   (`sized_qty=0` refusals, `PB-20260630-001`). **Still open, decoupled from this:**

   Built the tooling (per-fold AUC + a learned market-only P(win) ranker as a selection arm in
